## Continuing work-order sessions — provider-neutral team worker (2026-10-04)

- `task_session_sdk_turn.py` runs one claimed team turn on the session profile's current model account through the installed Agents SDK: Responses for OpenAI and the Codex account route, Chat Completions for Anthropic, Gemini, Grok, local Ollama, and cataloged compatible providers. The scoped team tools stay the only action authority. Each attempt records its provider and model, so changing an agent's account takes effect on its next turn without losing session history. An account that cannot run team tools (for example the Grok account route) fails the turn as `agent_model_unavailable`.
- The app's coordination launcher runs this worker in its own loop only when `TEAM_WORKER_ENABLED=true` (default off), with expiry and Chief decision dispatch, so a long turn does not delay mail or triage. Provider failures are classified without provider text: 429/503 and refused connections are retryable once; timeouts and dropped connections are unknown and never retried.
- Checks: Ruff, strict mypy, seven new unit tests for provider binding and failure classification, and 19 tests in `tests/test_task_session_postgres.py` against disposable PostgreSQL. The new test runs Chief on Ollama and Researcher on a compatible provider with scripted SDK models, then switches the Chief to the second provider between turns; delegation, result release, synthesis, recorded provider/model, and a rate-limited retry are read back. No live provider call was made. The Codex account route through the SDK is unproved live; installed team mode remains off.

## Continuing work-order sessions — hard team limits and one safe retry (2026-10-04)

- Migration `0067` and new settings bound automatic team work: delegation depth (default 2), assignments per work order (8), plan revisions (3), and automatic Chief decision turns per work order (6). A delegation over a bound saves a failed receipt with a `team_limit_*` reason and tells the agent to ask the operator. When the Chief decision bound is reached, the main session becomes `waiting_for_operator` with one `task_session_limit_reached` event; an operator message or Continue resumes it and consumes the waiting causes. Operator-started turns do not count toward the bound.
- A turn the provider provably never processed (for example HTTP 429, or the app-server failing before turn start) and that started no tool call is retried once after `TEAM_RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS` (default 30). The retry keeps the assignment active and does not wake the Chief. A second failure, a turn with any tool receipt, or an unknown outcome is never retried. Retry links and earliest-claim times are immutable.
- Checks: Ruff, strict mypy, release source verification, settings tests, and all 18 tests in `tests/test_task_session_postgres.py` against disposable PostgreSQL, including two new tests for every limit and the retry rules. No live provider or installed app change.

## Continuing work-order sessions — interrupted action and history recovery (2026-10-04)

- Before a new turn, the team runner reads back any previous tool receipt left `started` by an interrupted process. It settles the receipt from the canonical saved message, assignment, or result, or records that no local effect was found. The next prompt includes bounded confirmed action receipts. This prevents a new Codex thread from treating an already saved assignment or result as merely a prose proposal.
- When Codex reports that a saved thread has no local rollout, the adapter may create a replacement thread in the same OneFoundry session after those receipts are reconciled. A missing rollout does not close the work order or erase its public history. Focused disposable PostgreSQL tests covered confirmed and absent public updates, committed delegation and result reporting with interrupted receipts, a prior completed turn, second-process resume, and replacement after a missing rollout. ## Continuing work-order sessions — enqueue and attempt ledger (2026-10-04)

- Migration `0061_team_attempt_requests` separates legacy and team write modes, gives Continue an idempotency identity, and snapshots the message sequence when an attempt is claimed. Team Send stores a message and queues one attempt atomically with an immediate `202`; messages arriving during active work coalesce into one next attempt. Continue after an unknown or failed attempt queues a new attempt in the same session. The old text chat rejects new sends after team cutover, while historical reads remain available.
- The claim and settle owner keeps attempt outcome separate from work-order completion, records a public reply only after a confirmed successful turn, and carries later operator messages into one next attempt. The cutover field is database-owner only and defaults to `legacy`; no installed work order can enter this unfinished path through the UI. Live Codex dispatch, scoped Gateway actions, child scheduling, and UI are still required before cutover.
- Focused disposable PostgreSQL tests cover immediate acknowledgement, duplicate and stale sends, cross-project denial, old-chat write exclusion, coalescing, and continuation after an unknown attempt. Ruff, strict mypy, and `git diff --check` passed. The disposable test stack was removed; the installed runtime and user data were not changed.

- The separate Codex app-server adapter can advertise an explicitly admitted experimental tool namespace, scope calls to the active provider thread and turn, reject unlisted tools, and deduplicate repeated call IDs within a turn. It enables the experimental protocol only when a callback is supplied. This is an offline protocol component; no OneFoundry Gateway bridge, live Codex launch, or team-session cutover exists yet.
- Seven fake-server protocol tests pass, including admitted and denied tool calls. Ruff, strict mypy (438 source files), and `git diff --check` pass. No operator account, installed runtime, or work-order data was changed.

## Continuing work-order sessions — ledger slice (2026-10-04)

A rejected legacy assignment remains text history and creates no child assignment. The current Send and Chief follow-up paths are still active; no team execution is enabled by this slice.

## Continuing work-order session redesign (design only, 2026-10-04)

Viewing sibling logs and saving peer messages never wakes an agent. The Chief may arrange a targeted consultation for an unforeseen dependency, while target-versus-forecast completion cost guides dispatch decisions. This is a design correction, not installed messaging behavior.

## Independent versioned release and software update path (2026-09-30)

- Added OneFoundry `VERSION=0.1.0`, a release image override, offline prebuilt image bundle builder, and a Python operator command for check/install/start/stop/status/update/rollback. The bundle carries exact image IDs and SHA-256 file digests; apply requires a trusted archive SHA-256. The update stops its configured Compose project, checkpoints five named volumes, starts the candidate without a build or pull, checks readiness, and restores the prior release/data on a failed start. Manual rollback requires explicit data-restore flags because later writes are lost.

## OneFoundry Phase 1 — organization evidence (2026-09-28)

- Phase 0 was scoped-committed locally as `11e1e8b09d8c37bd0a5105b320edff477e35ec80`; no push. Phase 1 was then explicitly authorized. At Phase 1 start, the shared source had one Alembic head, `0036_unknown_live_restart`, so the additive migration is `0037_onefoundry_organizations` with that exact parent. - Task 2 adds a typed organization subject, an `off|records` setting defaulting to `off`, and five project/actor-scoped tables: organizations, sources, claims, workflows, and optional work-order subject links. Composite FKs keep source/claim/workflow relationships in the same project and organization. A partial unique index protects reviewed canonical domains; the link has a unique workflow step/revision key. Existing work orders require no backfill. Public sources require HTTPS plus a pasted excerpt and observed timestamp; input-file sources require a same-project ready file and its saved digest. Claims from input files must quote a saved extracted chunk and exact locator.
- Task 3 adds project-scoped list/detail/source/claim/review/qualification API routes in `records` only. Mutations use Tool Gateway entrypoints and append events with IDs/digests/decisions, never source text. Candidate, human review, verification, supersession, and derived freshness remain separate. Expected revisions reject stale source adds, claim proposals, reviews, and qualification. Approval advances the saved workflow to `research_reviewed`; a concurrent double review admits one decision. Invalid evidence and secret-like text fail validation; foreign project IDs return 404; stale or unsupported evidence returns 409.
- Task 4 adds an Organizations tab only in `records`, a deterministic next-action projection, and owner reread after each save. The records UI offers source, claim review, and qualification operations; no model, research order, draft, or outbound action was introduced. The first isolated browser startup could not bind occupied Mailpit port `18026`; changing only the disposable env to free port `18028` succeeded. Browser acceptance on isolated `onefoundry_phase1_browser` at `127.0.0.1:18766` created fictional Cedar Valley and Pine Lake districts, pasted excerpts, proposed and approved claims, qualified Cedar Valley, reloaded, and read the same saved state. ## Skill-tool order 5 continuation — browser timeout claim fence
- Made the no-claim timeout reserve the same exclusive claim path used by the host before recording `unknown`. If the host claims first, reconciliation waits for its receipt. ## Skill-tool order 8 — approved GitHub PR comment

Unknown outcomes have no automatic POST retry; status distinguishes a pending readback from a crash after readback began. Other connected actions remain unselected.

## Skill-tool order 4 — repository and coding evidence
- Added `coding_evidence_v1` to trusted role-tool admission. A profile must allow it both when the text run starts and when the call occurs. The read accepts one external-code run ID in the same project and returns at most three attempts with launch-time source bindings, status, corroborated receipt/test/commit/diff hashes, and at most two artifact metadata rows per attempt. It uses a stable PostgreSQL snapshot. A missing or mismatched append-only receipt event hides all receipt-derived fields and sets `receipt_event_verified=false`; `verified_outcome` stays empty unless a terminal receipt is corroborated. ## Offline fake coding CLI isolation proof
- Added a networkless Bubblewrap supervisor for controlled fake Codex and Claude executables. It runs only in a disposable fixture checkout with a private home. The child sees neither the host home, original repository, AgentPod secrets, receipt spool, nor Docker socket. Limits bound per-process address space, individual file size, per-UID process count, output, and wall time. A killed or uncertain attempt records `unknown` and cannot relaunch from the same claim.
- Both fake adapters produced schema-valid receipts with real fixture test results, a diff, hashed artifacts, session IDs, and reported usage. Focused tests passed 21/21. `make check` passed Ruff, strict mypy, 273 local tests, 94 disposable PostgreSQL tests, 108 frontend tests, lint, and build; `git diff --check` passed. Independent review found no P0/P1 blocker for this fake-only slice. Per-process limits are not aggregate container quotas, and the fake executable path is not fd-pinned; neither is accepted as a live coding-provider boundary.
- Check it in the browser: open `http://127.0.0.1:8879/`, select **Offline Probe Acceptance**, then **Coding Backends**. With no ready Engineer order, Codex CLI and Claude Code show **Not checked** and the page says a saved repository does not prove a live coding connection. Click **Open run in Activity** to see the existing saved offline probe receipt, diff, and passing check. This supervisor has no live launch button and makes no provider call; its new behavior is verified by `make check` from `agentpod/`.

## Codex expiry contract and verified account connection
- The operator completed a fresh OpenAI device verification. AgentPod saved the OAuth response but rejected its real `expires_in` value of 864000 seconds because its Codex parser imposed an unsupported 86400-second maximum. The access token's expiry agreed with the ten-day response. JintellarCore's Codex adapter does not impose that maximum. The old parser was changed to accept any positive integer lifetime while still rejecting malformed, boolean, zero, and negative values.
- The ten-day saved-exchange regression failed before the one-line parser fix and passed after it. ## Codex one-time exchange timeout and live follow-up
- A fresh operator device challenge reached OpenAI's device endpoint with `status=success`, while the saved AgentPod account remained pending. The current app's Finish route then returned HTTP 502 for an OAuth token exchange HTTP 400. The first exchange response was not captured, so a consumed code versus another OAuth rejection cannot yet be distinguished; this attempt does not prove live sign-in works.
- AgentPod had used the 5-second model-discovery timeout for the one-time OAuth token exchange and refresh. JintellarCore's equivalent timeout is 60 seconds; current Codex source explicitly warns that a response timeout can consume a one-time code. A regression test first failed at 5 seconds. The exchange now has its own validated 60-second default, with an explicit unknown-outcome error after transport failure and no automatic retry. The Models page clears its green automatic-connection notice if polling stops with an error or expiry.
- A clean-snapshot `make check` passed Ruff, strict mypy, 236 Python unit tests, 84 disposable PostgreSQL tests, 86 frontend tests, lint, and production build. Advancing the browser clock past code expiry produced the expiry error and removed the green notice. The isolated stack and its disposable volumes were removed. A new operator-verified code, connected-account readback, model discovery, and provider inference remain unverified; the shared local app has not been rebuilt for this slice.

## Codex token response compatibility follow-up
- The operator's new screenshot still showed the old manual Finish button. The running app served the newer hashed Models bundle, and a fresh mounted Firefox page showed automatic-completion copy with no Codex Finish button. An already open browser tab must reload to use that bundle.
- A verified device challenge returned HTTP 200 with an authorization code and valid PKCE pair; OpenAI's later OAuth exchange returned HTTP 400 with `token_exchange_user_error`. A prior Finish response had been the generic 409. AgentPod required `expires_in` from a successful token response, while the OpenAI Codex device-login test omits it and JintellarCore accepts a missing expiry. OpenAI's test also places the ChatGPT account ID in `id_token` with an opaque access token; AgentPod previously checked only the access token. Either mismatch could consume a one-time authorization code before saving the connection. The exact payload of the earlier successful exchange was not retained, so that causal link remains unproved.
- Codex credentials now accept a missing `expires_in`, use the access token's `exp` claim when present, and otherwise keep the expiry unknown. The account ID can come from `id_token` when the access token does not identify it. Model discovery and live work-order validation accept unknown expiry without an immediate refresh. The official-response-shape unit test failed before each fix and passed afterward. The complete scoped fix passed `make check` from a clean snapshot: Ruff, mypy, 234 Python unit tests, 84 disposable PostgreSQL tests, 86 frontend tests, lint, and build. In the shared checkout, `make check` remains blocked by unrelated in-progress source work: one formatting failure and six health tests whose fixtures lack that work's new secret directory; its disposable PostgreSQL suite passed all 84 tests and current frontend tests passed 91.
- A clean local image was built from committed app files plus only these three Codex source edits, then the localhost app was recreated. `/health/ready` returned 200. Mounted Firefox showed the expired Personal card with Start fresh sign-in and no Finish button; the console had no errors. The card fit at 390px and 320px. Fresh account verification, connected readback, model discovery, and provider inference still require the operator to verify a new code.

## Role tool admission — first slice
- Adapted a reviewed default-deny discovery/execution concept in AgentPod's Python Tool Gateway without copying its TypeScript gateway. An internal `current_work_order` read is registered for active synthetic/live text runs only when both the saved run snapshot and current profile allow it. Calls validate exact context, version, and empty JSON arguments; record intent before execution and a redacted outcome afterward; and deny duplicate call IDs. The read uses a read-only PostgreSQL transaction with bounded statements while row locks hold the active run, project, order, and profile through the result receipt. Its exact result shape and secret-field filter are checked before model delivery. Unknown receipt outcomes do not retry. Changed tool semantics require a new tool ID because run snapshots pin IDs.

## Codex subscription inference diagnosis (2026-09-29)

- Read-only review of the running app found a new GPT-6 Sol live-text attempt saved as `unknown` after roughly 15 seconds with only `incomplete_response`. The app discarded the terminal SSE event and provider error code, so the exact outcome of that historical call cannot be recovered from local records. The configured request timeout was 30 seconds; the error came from response interpretation, not that timeout path.
- Reviewed every module under JintellarCore `cloud-inference/account_runtime`, including the Codex adapter, credential client, refresh coordinator, model catalog, policy, errors, normalizers, and registry. AgentPod already uses the same legacy Codex CLI device client, ChatGPT backend catalog, and `backend-api/codex/responses` route. JintellarCore accumulates streamed text but can accept it without a `response.completed` terminal event. AgentPod will accumulate deltas only when the stream confirms completion.
- The saved AgentPod token was inspected read-only without printing credentials: its public API audience was present, but the direct ChatGPT plan inference scope was absent. OpenAI's current documented subscription flow uses a dynamically issued client, explicit `chatgpt.tokens.use.direct` grant, account-specific `/v1/models`, and public `/v1/responses`; the legacy device connection does not establish those permissions. Catalog discovery is therefore not proof of inference admission. A supported subscription sign-in migration and a fresh operator consent are required before claiming that path works.
- Source-only diagnostics now separate explicit `response.failed`, `response.incomplete`, refusal, and completed-without-text from a stream that ends without completion. Confirmed provider terminal failures become `failed`; an interrupted or unconfirmed stream remains `unknown`, never automatically retried. Task chat saves the same fixed diagnostic in its event and exposes it in the transcript. Models labels the Codex account as device signed in and warns that inference access is unverified. No provider body, token, prompt, or response text enters diagnostic events.
- The regular app was not rebuilt or restarted, and no further provider request was sent. The previous unknown attempt remains unchanged. The source changes have not been proven against a live subscription response.
- `make check` passed Ruff format/check, strict mypy (335 files), 673 local Python tests with one optional skip, 292 disposable PostgreSQL tests, the additional integration selections, frontend lint, 204 tests, and build. `git diff --check` passed. The new frontend build was served through a read-only local proxy to the existing API; browser readback confirmed the Codex account warning at 1280×720 and 390×844 with no visible clipping. The normal port 8765 app still serves its prior image. Live subscription sign-in, model discovery through the new grant, and a confirmed GPT-6 Sol response remain unexecuted gates.

## Offline broker TLS transport (2026-09-30)

- Added one-connection HTTP CONNECT and TLS transport to the offline broker. It checks the exact fixture destination, client SNI, request path and host, bounded headers/body/response, and an absolute session deadline. The broker replaces a placeholder client credential with its separate dummy secret only on a verified loopback `.test` provider connection. Equal credentials fail before network handling. A disconnect after egress marks the session unknown, and another request cannot replay it.

The independent review found no remaining blocker for this **offline** proof. The transport is not an installed service; the tests do not prove OS-level secret isolation, production network policy, real CLI compatibility, or a spending ceiling.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 1: signals and rules core (2026-09-30)

- Migration `0043_coordination_core` adds `coordination_signals`, versioned and immutable `coordination_rules`/`coordination_rule_versions`, per-project `coordination_controls`, and renames `onefoundry_intents` to the general `coordination_intents` in place. The qualified-organization → Sales continuation is now the built-in `onefoundry_draft_outreach` action in that queue and records an `organization.qualified` signal. Work orders gain `origin_signal_id` and `chain_depth`. Upgrade, downgrade to 0042, and re-upgrade were exercised on a disposable database.
- Verified state changes record internal signals in the same transaction: work order `done`, `needs_review`, `failed`, `unknown`, `blocked`, and approval `approved`/`rejected`. `COORDINATION_MODE` defaults to `off` (no signals); `draft_only` caps every rule at draft; `auto` honours a rule's "ready". No path starts a run. `COORDINATION_MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH` (1–30, default 10) and `COORDINATION_MAX_ORDERS_PER_DAY` (1–1000, default 50) are validated settings; rules may lower the chain limit and set their own daily cap. Blocked matches keep their reason (`chain_depth_exceeded`, `rule_daily_limit`, `project_daily_limit`).
- The coordinator service matches one signal and fulfils one rule intent per step with `SKIP LOCKED`, creating the work order and completing the intent in one transaction. It holds database access only and never calls a model. The Compose `onefoundry-event` profile was removed: the service starts by default and exits cleanly when neither business event mode nor coordination is enabled. A per-project pause stops new claims; events keep queuing.
- New **Automation** page: status, pause/resume, rule list and editor, and a recent-events feed explaining what each event did. Task details label automatically created work. Rule matching uses only allow-listed normalized fields; signal details are appended to instructions as labelled data.
- Checks: Ruff format/check and strict mypy (365 files) passed. 712 local Python unit tests passed with one optional skip after adding readiness stubs. The exact Compose PostgreSQL list (now including `tests/test_coordination_postgres.py`, 9 tests) passed 315/315 against a fresh disposable database run from the host, not inside the Compose test container. Frontend lint, type check, and 235 tests passed; production build succeeded. `git diff --check` passed. The Docker image/egress proofs in `check_postgres.sh` and the full `make check` were not run in this slice; two `test_skill_selection_unit.py` tests fail identically on unmodified `main` (see `docs/REMOVAL_CANDIDATES.md`).
- Browser: a local branch app (port 18891) with a disposable database and the coordinator in `draft_only` mode was driven in headless Chromium at 1280px and 390px. A rule created through the form with "Ready to start" produced a QA **draft** at chain step 1 after an Engineer order was accepted; the feed showed the created work order and "No rule matched" for the review-wait event; pause and resume worked; document width equalled the viewport and no console errors appeared. The installed app at port 8765 and its database were not touched.
- Dead-code notes from this slice are in `docs/REMOVAL_CANDIDATES.md`.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 2: schedules (2026-09-30)

- Migration `0044_coordination_schedules` adds a schedule to rule versions and checks that only `schedule` rules carry one. A rule's source is now derived from its signal type. Schedules run every day or week at a set time in `COORDINATION_TIMEZONE` (IANA name, default `UTC`, validated at startup), or every 1–168 hours.
- The coordinator records one `schedule.fired` signal per due time, deduplicated by rule and slot, before matching. Nothing fires for slots before the rule version was saved; after a pause or restart only the most recent missed slot fires; paused or inactive projects fire nothing. A schedule signal can only match the rule that fired it. Created work carries the scheduled time as quoted data.
- Automation's rule form adds "On a schedule" with daily, weekly, or hourly controls and describes saved schedules in the configured time zone.
- Checks: Ruff and strict mypy (368 files) passed. New unit tests cover due-time math, time zones, missed-slot collapse, and schedule validation; local unit selection 722 passed (two settings tests fail only when this host's private path variables are exported, and pass without them). The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 316/316 on a fresh database from the host, including a schedule test for early, paused, due, and duplicate firing. Frontend lint, type check, 240 tests, and build passed.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 3: agent findings and Chief of Staff triage (2026-09-30)

- New role tool `report_signal_v1` (default-off; needs saved and current allowlists) records one finding — a topic from a fixed list plus a bounded summary — as an `agent.finding` signal with run lineage and the reporting work order's chain depth. It is refused while `COORDINATION_MODE=off`. Rules can match findings by topic or agent.
- Migration `0045_coordination_triage` adds the `triage` signal state, `chief_triage` intents (one per signal), saved decisions, and a standing triage work order per project. Unmatched signals from findings and future external sources queue triage; internal status events without a rule are still ignored. Triage respects the chain limit and counts toward the project's daily cap.
- A new in-app launcher (actor `coordination-launcher`) claims one triage at a time, opens one Task chat per event on the project's **Automation triage** Chief of Staff work order, and sends one bounded turn through the existing Task chat path, so credentials, prompt limits, the job dossier, and unknown-outcome handling are reused. The event is quoted as data. Only one JSON decision is accepted (create one draft for a named agent, ignore, or ask the operator); anything else asks the operator. Triage drafts are always drafts. A rejected call blocks the event; an uncertain or interrupted call becomes unknown and is never retried. The coordinator service still holds no credentials.
- Automation shows findings, "With Chief of Staff", and each triage decision with its reason, and the rule form can filter findings by topic.
- Checks: Ruff and strict mypy (373 files) passed; local unit selection 724 passed with one optional skip; the Compose PostgreSQL list passed 321/321 on a fresh database from the host, including 5 triage tests with a fake model (create, rule precedence, malformed reply, unknown not retried, chain limit and missing Chief model, tool refused when off). Frontend lint, type check, 241 tests, and build passed. No live model call was made.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 4: read-only IMAP mail (2026-09-30)

- Migration `0046_mail_accounts` adds per-project mailbox connections and permits a future `document` signal source. Passwords are write-only and live in the protected `mail` subdirectory of `MODEL_SECRET_ROOT`; the API returns only credential presence.
- `mail_imap.py` uses Python `imaplib` over implicit TLS with certificate verification. It opens folders with `EXAMINE` and fetches with `BODY.PEEK[]<0.N>`, so reading never sets flags; it never sends. Failures map to fixed codes (authentication, connection, TLS, folder, search, fetch).
- **Check connection** verifies sign-in, capabilities (including `IDLE`), and the folder, and sets the starting point after the newest existing message; a mailbox can be turned on only after a successful check. The in-app launcher polls each enabled mailbox every `MAIL_POLL_SECONDS` while automation is on and the project is active and unpaused. Each new message becomes one `email.received` signal deduplicated by server, folder, `UIDVALIDITY`, and UID, with bounded normalized fields, a snippet, and the bounded original saved mode 0600 with its SHA-256 under the artifact volume. A changed `UIDVALIDITY` restarts after the newest message without backfill; a rejected password turns the mailbox off.
- Rules can match email by sender domain, sender address, or mailbox; unmatched email goes to Chief of Staff triage. Automation gains a **Mail inboxes** section with Zoho setup guidance (imap.zoho.com or regional host, port 993, IMAP enabled, app-specific password).
- Checks: Ruff and strict mypy (381 files) passed; local unit selection 729 passed with one optional skip, including 5 tests against a local TLS IMAP server that record every command (read-only, ordering, batching, truncation, wrong password, unreachable server, untrusted certificate). The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 322/322 on a fresh database from the host, including an end-to-end mail test (account API, duplicate label, enable-before-check refused, rule match to a Sales draft with an injection attempt kept as quoted data, unmatched mail to triage, replay without duplicates, `UIDVALIDITY` rebuild, rotated password). Frontend lint, type check, 242 tests, and build passed. **Live acceptance against the operator's Zoho mailbox has not been run**; it needs the operator's app password entered in the UI.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 5: automatic starts (2026-09-30)

- Migration `0047_coordination_auto_start` adds the `auto_start` rule autonomy and one launch intent per created work order, and narrows the one-intent-per-rule uniqueness to rule intents.
- In `COORDINATION_MODE=auto` only, an auto-start rule creates ready work plus a launch. The in-app launcher (`coordination_autostart.py`) starts one live text run through the existing gateway path, so the assigned agent's model binding, prompt limits, tool preflight, deliverable evaluation, and review waits all apply; a model reply alone still leaves operator-review work in `needs_review`. Launches stop at `COORDINATION_MAX_AUTO_STARTS_PER_DAY` (default 20), when the project is paused or inactive, when the work order changed, or when the mode is no longer `auto`; blocked work stays ready for the operator. Rejected runs block the launch; uncertain or interrupted runs become unknown and are never retried. Triage drafts never auto-start.
- Automation adds "Start automatically", explains how each rule behaves in the current mode, shows the auto-start cap, and reports each launch outcome.
- Checks: Ruff and strict mypy (384 files) passed; local unit selection 729 passed with one optional skip. The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 325/325 on a fresh database from the host, including 3 auto-start tests with a fake model (one run per launch and `needs_review` afterwards; drafts-only never launches; daily cap, unknown outcome not retried, and an agent without a model). Frontend lint, type check, 243 tests, and build passed. No live model call was made.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 6: Chief-proposed agents (2026-09-30)

- Migration `0048_agent_proposals` adds a per-project tool ceiling and agent proposals bound to approval requests. `propose_profile_v1` (built-in Chief of Staff only; saved and current allowlists) validates name, instructions, memory scope, autonomy (manual or supervised), optional reuse of the Chief's model, an optional first draft work order, and requested tools, which must be inside the operator's ceiling. It records one pending approval whose content is a readable summary and whose digest is the SHA-256 of the exact proposal.
- Approving in the existing inbox re-checks the digest and the ceiling, then creates exactly the proposed custom agent under the Chief and its draft work order in the same transaction as the decision; a narrowed ceiling or taken name returns 409 and leaves the approval pending. Rejecting creates nothing. Automation adds a tool-ceiling editor listing registered Tool Gateway IDs.
- Checks: Ruff and strict mypy (387 files) passed; local unit selection 729 passed with one optional skip. The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 327/327 on a fresh database from the host, including 2 proposal tests (pending until approved, exact agent and first draft created, ceiling and role refusal, rejection, ceiling narrowed before approval, unknown tool in ceiling). The test marks a manual run as an in-flight live run with the database owner login, because role tools only run inside model runs. Frontend lint, type check, 243 tests, and build passed.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 7: Microsoft 365 and Google sign-in, API mailboxes (2026-10-01)

- Migration `0049_connector_accounts` adds operator-registered Microsoft and Google accounts and lets mailboxes use `gmail` or `graph` through a connected account, with a provider change cursor.
- `connector_oauth.py` talks only to fixed official endpoints through the existing bounded HTTP transport (no proxies, no redirects): Microsoft device-code sign-in for a public client; Google installed-app authorization code with PKCE and a loopback redirect to the local app (the SPA forwards `?code&state` to the API). Scopes are the narrowest per selected feature. Tokens refresh automatically; a rejected refresh marks the account as needing sign-in. Client secrets, pending sign-in material, and tokens are stored base64-encoded in the protected `connectors` store; mail app passwords are now base64-encoded too so passwords with spaces work.
- `mail_api_sources.py` reads Gmail history (`messagesAdded` in INBOX, `format=raw`) and Graph inbox delta (skipping removals, `/$value` MIME), rejects stored cursors outside the Graph origin, and maps 401/403 and expired cursors to fixed codes. The connection check sets the starting cursor without importing history; an expired cursor rebaselines.
- Automation adds a Microsoft and Google accounts section with registration guidance (including Google's seven-day Testing-mode limit) and lets a mailbox use IMAP, Gmail, or Microsoft 365.
- Checks: Ruff (one import-order fix applied after the run) and strict mypy (396 files) passed; local unit selection 733 passed with one optional skip, including OAuth and mail-API tests against a scripted transport (device flow pending/success/declined, invalid tenant, PKCE URL, code exchange, refresh and rejection, missing refresh token; Gmail history, burst skip, expired cursor, 401; Graph delta paging, removals, foreign cursor). The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 328/328 on a fresh database from the host, including an end-to-end account test (Google without secret refused, Microsoft device sign-in, tokens absent from the API, Graph mailbox check and poll to an `email.received` signal, refresh of an expired token, removal blocked while a mailbox uses the account, Google PKCE start, forged callback refused). Frontend lint, type check, 245 tests, and build passed. **No live Microsoft or Google sign-in has been performed**; it needs the operator's own app registrations.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 8: watched drive folders (2026-10-01)

- Migration `0050_document_sources` adds watched folders (one folder in one connected account, with a provider change cursor) and per-file revision tracking. `document_api_sources.py` resolves OneDrive/SharePoint paths or Google Drive folder links, reads Graph drive delta and Drive changes, keeps only files directly inside the selected folder, downloads Graph files through `@microsoft.graph.downloadUrl` restricted to Microsoft content hosts, and exports Google Docs, Sheets, and Slides to DOCX, XLSX, and PPTX.
- `InputFileStore.ingest` saves fetched bytes through the existing input-file path (shared with uploads), so quotas, supported formats, and the restricted extraction worker are unchanged. Each new revision becomes one `document.created` or `document.changed` signal; unsupported or oversized files become signals with an ingest error. Work created from a document signal by a rule or by triage gets the input file linked, so the extracted text reaches the assigned agent. Unmatched document events are ignored rather than triaged.
- Automation adds a Watched folders section and a folder filter on document rules.
- Checks: Ruff (two test line-length fixes applied after the run) and strict mypy (403 files) passed; local unit selection 735 passed with one optional skip, including folder adapter tests (direct-children filter, deletions and subfolders skipped, download host allowlist, size limit, foreign cursor; Drive link parsing, parent filter, trashed and removed skipped, native export). The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 329/329 on a fresh database from the host, including an end-to-end watched-folder test (file access required, folder check, new file to input file and `document.created`, unsupported file recorded with an ingest error, rule-created work with the file linked, same revision ignored, new revision as `document.changed`). Frontend lint, type check, 246 tests, and build passed. No live drive access was performed.

## Event-driven coordination — Slice 9: approval-bound document writes (2026-10-01)

- Migration `0051_document_writes` stores each proposed write with its exact content and SHA-256. The role tool `propose_document_write_v1` (saved and current allowlists) accepts one new text, Markdown, or CSV file of at most 64 KiB for a ready watched folder whose account has `files_write`, rejects credential-shaped content, and creates a `publish` approval showing the content, with a digest binding source, folder, name, type, and content hash.
- The inbox decision re-checks that digest in the same transaction. Dispatch is a separate explicit operator action that moves an approved write to `dispatching` before any network call, creates the file as new (OneDrive renames on conflict, Google multipart upload), and reads it back: Google by MD5 and size, OneDrive by size. Provider errors mark it failed; lost connections and readback mismatches mark it unknown; nothing is ever sent twice. Editing existing files, sheet ranges, and sharing are not implemented.
- Watched folders lists proposed writes with plain-language states and a **Write exact file once** button for approved writes.
- Checks: Ruff and strict mypy (406 files) passed; local unit selection 735 passed with one optional skip. The Compose PostgreSQL list passed 330/330 on a fresh database from the host, including an end-to-end write test (write access required, approval content and hash, dispatch refused before approval, approved write created and verified by MD5 and size, second dispatch refused, rejected write refused, readback mismatch as unknown, lost connection as unknown and not resent). Frontend lint, type check, 247 tests, and build passed. No live drive write was performed.

# Outgoing SMTP release 0.2.1 (2026-10-03)

- Added a project-scoped outgoing SMTP control in Automation, separate from inbound mail. Credentials are write-only in the protected secret volume; TLS is mandatory. The operator checks and enables an account before use.
- A running manual work order or a run/profile-admitted `propose_email_v1` tool can create one exact plain-text email proposal. Each requires its own operator approval; approval triggers one SMTP submission attempt with a committed claim, and unknown outcomes never retry automatically. SMTP acceptance is recorded separately from recipient delivery.
- `make check` completed: source contract, Ruff formatting/lint, strict mypy, 708 local Python tests with one existing skip, 4 focused SMTP unit tests, 332 disposable PostgreSQL tests, Docker integration proofs, 248 frontend tests, lint, and production build. A later focused database rerun passed both SMTP tests after adding auth-failure, concurrent approval, and run-timeline receipt checks.
- Disposable localhost app on port 18893 migrated to `0052_outbound_smtp` and returned ready. Its OpenAPI exposed all four outgoing mail route groups. A real in-app browser showed **Automation → Outgoing mail**, the separate IMAP/SMTP explanation, disabled compose action without an account and running work order, and the SMTP form at desktop and 390 px mobile width; no browser console errors. The disposable stack and volumes were removed afterward. No real SMTP account or credential was used; live provider acceptance and recipient delivery remain unverified.

## Agent-authored Automation from project chats — working-tree slice (2026-10-03)

- Migration `0053_conversation_origin` adds immutable Task chat source lineage for app chat and confirmed Discord/WeChat DMs, a per-project agent rule switch, and one turn- or live text run-bound decision receipt. Normal project chats and agent work-order text runs ask their assigned agent for one structured decision in the existing model call. The Tool Gateway validates and saves rule create/revise/disable or a versioned project goal update, then reads back the result. Chief chat can save a draft child assignment for another agent; the child can create the rule on its own run. System triage retains its separate exact response contract. Rule listing shows the last agent action and its source turn or run. Agent writes have profile actor IDs; outbound approvals and coordinator mode still apply.
- The operator chose a separate project-wide plan record. Migration `0054_project_plan` seeds a version-1 record for existing projects; new projects seed one on creation. `GET/PATCH /api/projects/{id}/plan` and Task chat Assignment let the operator read or edit it with optimistic version checks. Chat and live-text agent decisions can update the plan alone or save a plan, project goal, and one rule command atomically. Receipts record the plan version and actual profile actor. Chief chat can assign a draft child to another agent; a started child live-text run can then create the rule. Historical Chief triage conversations are backfilled as system-origin turns by `0053`.
- Migration `0055_chief_assignment_launch` lets a Chief decision request `delegate_autonomy=auto_start`. In Auto mode it saves a ready child and one launch intent; the existing bounded launcher claims it and starts the assigned agent. Otherwise the child stays draft.
